+    def test_adr0043_ac07_binary_signature_covers_opencode_codex_and_claude(self):
+        """Generalizes the pre-existing opencode-only binary signature (ADR-0034 AC-08)
+        to `codex`/`claude` too -- a binary swap (mtime change) must move the signature,
+        and an unresolvable binary must fail closed to a fixed per-name sentinel."""
+        from routing_core import catalog as cat
+        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
+            path = Path(td) / "codex"; path.write_text("#!/bin/sh\n"); path.chmod(0o755)
+            with mock.patch.object(cat.shutil, "which", side_effect=lambda name: str(path) if name == "codex" else None):
+                present = cat._binary_signature("codex")
+                self.assertNotEqual(present, "codex:absent")
+                older = os.stat(path)
+                os.utime(path, (older.st_atime, older.st_mtime + 5))
+                bumped = cat._binary_signature("codex")
+                self.assertNotEqual(present, bumped)
+                self.assertEqual(cat._binary_signature("claude"), "claude:absent")
+
+    def test_adr0043_ac07_cache_key_folds_every_runtime_signature(self):
+        """`_cache_key` is a SINGLE hash covering the whole probe-cache document (every
+        runtime's pairs at once) -- a change in ANY one of the six new signatures must
+        move the overall key, exactly like the pre-existing opencode-only binary
+        signature already did."""
+        from routing_core import catalog as cat
+        base = cat._cache_key(self.config)
+        with mock.patch.object(cat, "_codex_auth_signature", return_value="different-codex"):
+            self.assertNotEqual(cat._cache_key(self.config), base)
+        with mock.patch.object(cat, "_claude_code_auth_signature", return_value="different-claude"):
+            self.assertNotEqual(cat._cache_key(self.config), base)
+        with mock.patch.object(cat, "_pi_auth_signature", return_value="different-pi"):
+            self.assertNotEqual(cat._cache_key(self.config), base)
+        # Confirms _binary_signature is actually invoked for all THREE binaries, not
+        # merely that mocking it changes the result (a name-blind mock could pass even
+        # if `_cache_key` only ever called it for "opencode", the pre-existing call).
+        calls = []
+        original = cat._binary_signature
+        def spy(name):
+            calls.append(name)
+            return original(name)
+        with mock.patch.object(cat, "_binary_signature", side_effect=spy):
+            cat._cache_key(self.config)
+        self.assertEqual(sorted(calls), ["claude", "codex", "opencode"])
+        with mock.patch.object(cat, "_binary_signature", side_effect=lambda name: f"{name}:forced-different"):
+            self.assertNotEqual(cat._cache_key(self.config), base)
+
+    def test_adr0043_ac07_cache_key_never_spawns_a_subprocess_for_the_new_signatures(self):
+        """Trap #2 (context pack): AC-07 adds six new stat/local-read signatures to
+        `_cache_key` without a single new subprocess -- the ONE subprocess the whole
+        cache-key computation ever pays is `_live_opencode_auth_signature`, called by the
+        CALLER (`probe_inventory`) and passed in as `auth_signature`, never invoked from
+        `_cache_key` itself (the no-argument call below never touches it)."""
+        from routing_core import catalog as cat
+        def fail(*a, **k):
+            raise AssertionError("no subprocess may run while computing _cache_key")
+        with mock.patch.object(cat.subprocess, "run", side_effect=fail):
+            cat._cache_key(self.config)  # must not raise
+
+    def test_adr0043_ac07_ac08_codex_refresh_keeps_the_cache_warm_and_logout_invalidates_it_via_probe_inventory(self):
+        """End-to-end AC-08 property proof, through `probe_inventory` itself (never a
+        real `codex logout` -- the credential file is deleted directly, same discipline
+        the task instructions require): a refresh keeps the cache warm (codex is never
+        reprobed); a logout misses the cache on the VERY NEXT call, still well inside
+        PROBE_CACHE_TTL (300s) -- unlike before this package, where only opencode's
+        credential change was ever visible before the TTL expired."""
+        from routing_core import catalog as cat
+        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
+            bins, log = self._probe_stubs(td)
+            home = Path(td) / "home"; (home / ".codex").mkdir(parents=True)
+            auth = home / ".codex" / "auth.json"
+            auth.write_text(json.dumps({"tokens": {"account_id": "acct_1", "access_token": "before"}}))
+            cache_root = Path(td) / "root"; cache_root.mkdir(mode=0o700)
+            old_path = os.environ["PATH"]; os.environ["PATH"] = f"{bins}:{old_path}"
+            try:
+                with mock.patch.object(cat.Path, "home", return_value=home):
+                    cold = cat.probe_inventory(self.config, cache_root=cache_root, now=1000.0)
+                    self.assertIn(("codex", "openai-codex"), cold)
+                    auth.write_text(json.dumps({"tokens": {"account_id": "acct_1", "access_token": "AFTER"}}))
+                    log.write_text("")
+                    warm = cat.probe_inventory(self.config, cache_root=cache_root, now=1001.0)
+                    self.assertEqual(warm, cold)
+                    self.assertNotIn("bin/codex", log.read_text())
+                    auth.unlink()
+                    log.write_text("")
+                    cat.probe_inventory(self.config, cache_root=cache_root, now=1002.0)
+                    self.assertIn("bin/codex", log.read_text())
+            finally:
+                os.environ["PATH"] = old_path
+
+    def test_adr0043_ac09_credential_signatures_fail_closed_on_symlink_and_corrupt_or_foreign_shaped_json(self):
+        """Same discipline as `test_pi_auth_provider_keys_reads_names_only_and_fails_closed`
+        above, extended to the two new credential readers: a symlink is never followed,
+        and corrupt/foreign-shaped JSON never raises -- always the empty signature."""
+        from routing_core import catalog as cat
+        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
+            home = Path(td)
+            (home / ".codex").mkdir(); (home / ".claude").mkdir()
+            codex_auth = home / ".codex" / "auth.json"
+            claude_creds = home / ".claude" / ".credentials.json"
+            with mock.patch.object(cat.Path, "home", return_value=home):
+                self.assertEqual(cat._codex_auth_signature(), "")
+                self.assertEqual(cat._claude_code_auth_signature(), "")
+                real = home / "real-secret.json"
+                real.write_text(json.dumps({"tokens": {"account_id": "x"}}))
+                codex_auth.symlink_to(real)
+                self.assertEqual(cat._codex_auth_signature(), "")
+                codex_auth.unlink()
+                codex_auth.write_text("not json at all")
+                claude_creds.write_text("not json at all")
+                self.assertEqual(cat._codex_auth_signature(), "")
+                self.assertEqual(cat._claude_code_auth_signature(), "")
+                codex_auth.write_text(json.dumps(["not", "a", "dict"]))
+                claude_creds.write_text(json.dumps(["not", "a", "dict"]))
+                self.assertEqual(cat._codex_auth_signature(), "")
+                self.assertEqual(cat._claude_code_auth_signature(), "")
+
+    def test_adr0043_ac09_cache_schema_version_bump_invalidates_old_cache_documents(self):
+        """AC-09: bumping `_CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION` must change `_cache_key`'s output, and a
+        cache document written and keyed under the OLD version must never read back as
+        valid once the constant has moved -- there was no test for this before this
+        package (context pack: "hoy no existe ninguno")."""
+        from routing_core import catalog as cat
+        # ADR-0043 AC-09: the bump actually happened (2 -> 3) -- this literal pins that a
+        # future change reverting it (or forgetting a NEW bump when the key semantics
+        # move again) is caught here, not only by the relative comparison below.
+        self.assertEqual(cat._CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION, 3)
+        with mock.patch.object(cat, "_CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION", cat._CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION - 1):
+            old_key = cat._cache_key(self.config)
+        new_key = cat._cache_key(self.config)
+        self.assertNotEqual(old_key, new_key)
+        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
+            cache_root = Path(td) / "root"; cache_root.mkdir(mode=0o700)
+            doc = {"key": old_key, "at": 1000.0, "pairs": {}}
+            path = cache_root / "probe-cache.json"; path.write_text(json.dumps(doc)); path.chmod(0o600)
+            self.assertIsNone(cat._read_probe_cache(cache_root, new_key, 1000.5, self.config))
+
+    def test_adr0043_ac10_prune_legacy_probe_cache_removes_only_the_validated_sibling_file(self):
+        from routing_core import catalog as cat
+        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
+            root = Path(td) / "legacy"; root.mkdir(mode=0o700)
+            legacy = root / "probe-cache.json"
+            legacy.write_text(json.dumps({"key": "x", "at": 1.0, "pairs": {}}))
+            os.chmod(legacy, 0o600)
+            sibling = root / "config.toml"; sibling.write_text("untouched = true\n")
+            self.assertTrue(cat.prune_legacy_probe_cache(root))
+            self.assertFalse(legacy.exists())
+            self.assertTrue(sibling.exists())  # never touches anything else in the directory
+            self.assertTrue(root.exists())  # never removes the directory itself
+            self.assertFalse(cat.prune_legacy_probe_cache(root))  # idempotent no-op
+
+    def test_adr0043_ac10_prune_legacy_probe_cache_never_crosses_a_symlink_or_a_wrong_mode_directory(self):
+        """Same `_validate_cache_dir`/file-validation discipline as `_write_probe_cache`
+        (`test_probe_cache_ignores_unvalidated_directory` above) -- mirrored exactly for
+        the pruning path, in both directions (a symlinked/wrong-mode directory, and a
+        symlinked file inside an otherwise-valid one)."""
+        from routing_core import catalog as cat
+        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
+            real = Path(td) / "real"; real.mkdir(mode=0o700)
+            (real / "probe-cache.json").write_text("{}"); os.chmod(real / "probe-cache.json", 0o600)
+            link = Path(td) / "link"; link.symlink_to(real)
+            self.assertFalse(cat.prune_legacy_probe_cache(link))
+            self.assertTrue((real / "probe-cache.json").exists())
+            wrong_mode = Path(td) / "wrong"; wrong_mode.mkdir(mode=0o755)
+            (wrong_mode / "probe-cache.json").write_text("{}"); os.chmod(wrong_mode / "probe-cache.json", 0o600)
+            self.assertFalse(cat.prune_legacy_probe_cache(wrong_mode))
+            self.assertTrue((wrong_mode / "probe-cache.json").exists())
+            valid = Path(td) / "valid"; valid.mkdir(mode=0o700)
+            target = Path(td) / "target-secret.json"; target.write_text("{}")
+            (valid / "probe-cache.json").symlink_to(target)
+            self.assertFalse(cat.prune_legacy_probe_cache(valid))
+            self.assertTrue(target.exists())
+

+    def test_adr0042_ac01b_model_preference_providers_is_guarded_against_the_real_source(self):
+        # AC-01b: before this ADR, the ONLY assertion touching `_MODEL_PREFERENCE_PROVIDERS`
+        # lived at test_adr0034_ac09_write_cli_validates_pins_against_the_effective_set_
+        # not_just_the_constant (below, :3965 pre-refactor line count) -- inside a
+        # `mock.patch` block whose real purpose is testing the live-probe degrade path, and
+        # it compared against ANOTHER hardcoded literal, never against the audited source.
+        # That test is untouched by this package (still passes, proven by the full-suite
+        # gate) and still is not the guard -- THIS is the guard: two independent
+        # cross-checks against the real source, neither of them a second copy-pasted tuple.
+        #
+        # Cross-check 1: same pattern as test_adr0034_ac10_discoverable_providers_lockstep_
+        # guard above (which only ever covered DISCOVERABLE_PROVIDERS, never this tuple,
+        # despite set_agents_app.py's own comment claiming otherwise pre-ADR-0042) --
+        # membership against `_PAIR_COMMANDS`'s own audited provider set.
+        pair_providers = {provider for _, provider in routing_catalog._PAIR_COMMANDS}
+        self.assertEqual(set(set_agents_app._MODEL_PREFERENCE_PROVIDERS), pair_providers)
+        # Cross-check 2 (P1 repair, finding F01): comparing against `tuple(provider_
+        # registry.PROVIDERS)` directly, as this test did before the repair, WAS the
+        # false-green this package exists to close -- both sides re-derive from the SAME
+        # dict, so renaming, adding, removing, or reordering a `PROVIDERS` entry moves them
+        # together and the assertion stays green no matter what. The independent check is a
+        # hand-pinned literal of the audited universe instead, deliberately NOT imported or
+        # derived from `provider_registry`: a TEST is allowed to hardcode the universe it
+        # audits (that is its job -- fail noisily the moment someone changes it, on purpose
+        # or not); only PRODUCTION code must never hand-maintain a second copy. Do not "fix"
+        # this by deriving the literal below from PROVIDERS again.
+        self.assertEqual(set_agents_app._MODEL_PREFERENCE_PROVIDERS,
+                          ("openai-codex", "anthropic", "opencode-zen", "opencode-go"))

    36	# the bare `pi` on PATH (the managed `~/.local/bin/pi` wrapper only soft-pins by release
    37	# age via PNPM_CONFIG_MINIMUM_RELEASE_AGE) — the probe below and the T-303 spawner share
    38	# this ONE invocation builder, so the audited pair and the executed child are provably the
    39	# same binary. Bump PI_PINNED_VERSION deliberately (code review), never silently.
    40	PI_PACKAGE = "@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent"
    41	PI_PINNED_VERSION = "0.84.0"
    42	
    43	# PKG-N02 (repair R1): `set_agents_app.py --doctor --harness pi` already allows 60s
    44	# (DOCTOR_TIMEOUT_SECONDS in set_agents_spawn.py) for a cold `pnpm dlx` resolution on the
    45	# FIRST invocation of the pinned version in a given pnpm store. `probe_inventory`'s own
    46	# default timeout (20s, chosen for the other three runtimes' fast local CLI probes) was
    47	# never raised to match, so a genuinely cold store could silently time out the pi probe
    48	# and produce a false PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED until the store warmed — this floor closes
    49	# that gap for the pi pairs specifically, without slowing down the other (already-fast)
    50	# probes that share the same `timeout` parameter.
    51	PI_PROBE_MIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 60.0
    52	
    53	
    54	def pi_pinned_argv(*args: str) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    55	    """Exact-version-pinned `pi` invocation shared by the probe (here) and set_agents_spawn.
    56	
    57	    Deliberately NO `--` separator before `args`: live QA (2026-07-27) found `pnpm dlx
    58	    --package <pkg> pi -- <args>` forwards that `--` VERBATIM into pi's own argument
    59	    parser instead of stripping it — `pi --version`/`pi --list-models` tolerate the stray
    60	    token (both short-circuit on the first recognized early-exit flag regardless of what
    61	    else is on the line), which silently masked the bug for the probe/doctor calls, but a
    62	    real spawn invocation (`--model ... --print ...`) fails hard with `Unknown option: --`.
    63	    `pnpm dlx --package <pkg> <bin> <args...>` (no separator) is the correct form."""
    64	    return ("pnpm", "dlx", "--package", f"{PI_PACKAGE}@{PI_PINNED_VERSION}", "pi", *args)
    65	
    66	
    67	# T-305: catalog model id -> Pi canonical model id. openai-codex is IDENTITY (spike-verified
    68	# live, 2026-07-27: catalog `gpt-5.6-luna|sol|terra` == Pi's raw id, zero translation).
    69	# anthropic short catalog names need this curated map (spike-verified against a live
    70	# `pi --list-models`, aligned with the harness's own Claude tiers: auditors=opus-4.8,
    71	# implement=sonnet-5, mechanical=haiku-4.5) — the ONLY translation P3 needs, user-adjustable
    72	# if Pi's catalog names move.
    73	PI_MODEL_MAP = {"anthropic": {"opus": "claude-opus-4-8", "sonnet": "claude-sonnet-5", "haiku": "claude-haiku-4-5"}}
    74	
    75	# Repair SEC-001 (panel RP-01, security-auditor, critical, 012 repair): AC-07's
    76	# family-collision guard originally keyed `seen` on the RAW `model` string, so the SAME
    77	# underlying model curated under two DIFFERENT ids for two different providers (not the
    78	# same id under two providers, which AC-07's rule already catches) never collided at all
    79	# — e.g. `anthropic`/`opus` and a future `opencode-zen`/`claude-opus-4-8` row are the
    80	# identical model (proven in-repo by PI_MODEL_MAP above) but two different `model`
    81	# strings, so the old rule's `model in seen` check was always False for that pair.
    82	# CANONICAL_MODEL is a curated `(provider, model) -> canonical id` map, seeded from
    83	# PI_MODEL_MAP (the only other place in this file that already asserts this exact
    84	# identity), so `canonical_model` below resolves both spellings to the same key. Absent
    85	# from this map, a model id is already its own canonical form — this never invents an
    86	# equivalence beyond what is explicitly curated here, same discipline as PROVIDER_BILLING_KIND.
    87	#
    88	# Repair SEC-002 (delta-review round 2, medium): PI_MODEL_MAP is Pi's own CLI-invocation
    89	# name-translation table (a different purpose and lifecycle from a security guarantee —
    90	# see the comment at PI_MODEL_MAP's own definition above), and it does not cover every
    91	# Anthropic id models.toml's `[catalog].claude` allowlist curates: `fable` has no Pi
    92	# route, so PI_MODEL_MAP never needed to translate it, but `[catalog].opencode_zen`
    93	# still curates the SAME underlying model under the alias `claude-fable-5` — seeding
    94	# CANONICAL_MODEL from PI_MODEL_MAP alone left exactly this one pair uncurated, reopening
    95	# the SEC-001 hole for the single Anthropic model PI_MODEL_MAP doesn't translate.
    96	# _ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL_EXTRA is the explicit, hand-curated completion for ids PI_MODEL_MAP
    97	# has no reason to cover — never derived from PI_MODEL_MAP or any other table with a
    98	# different purpose. The coherence test (test_sec002_... in tests/test_routing.py)
    99	# asserts every `[catalog].claude` id resolves, through canonical_model, to a canonical
   100	# id actually present in `[catalog].opencode_zen`, so a future fifth Anthropic id added
   101	# to the allowlist without a curated alias here fails the suite instead of silently
   102	# reopening this same hole again.
   103	_ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL_EXTRA = {"fable": "claude-fable-5"}
   104	_ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL: dict[str, str] = {**PI_MODEL_MAP["anthropic"], **_ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL_EXTRA}
   105	CANONICAL_MODEL: dict[tuple[str, str], str] = {("anthropic", short): canonical
   106	                                               for short, canonical in _ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL.items()}
   107	CANONICAL_MODEL.update({(provider, canonical): canonical
   108	                        for provider in ("opencode-zen", "opencode-go")
   109	                        for canonical in _ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL.values()})
   110	
   111	
   112	def canonical_model(provider: str, model: str) -> str:
   113	    """Curated cross-provider model-identity normalization (repair SEC-001). Resolves a
   114	    (provider, model) pair to a canonical id ONLY where CANONICAL_MODEL explicitly
   115	    curates the two as the same underlying model; every other pair maps to its own
   116	    `model` string unchanged (identity), so this can never widen equivalence beyond what
   117	    a human curated. Consumed by `_check_family_collisions` below (layer 1) and by
   118	    `service.py`'s REVIEW_MODEL_CONFLICT hard exclusion (layer 2, defense in depth)."""
   119	    return CANONICAL_MODEL.get((provider, model), model)
   120	
   121	
   122	# ADR-0042 (022 PKG-1): both maps below DERIVE from the single `PROVIDERS` registry now
   123	# (`provider_registry.py`) -- before this ADR they were two independently hand-edited
   124	# literals, one of six provider-keyed tables that had to move in lockstep by hand across
   125	# this file, `models_config.py`, and `set_agents_app.py`. Values are unchanged from the
   126	# pre-ADR-0042 literals (PKG-1 AC-02 characterization test pins this).
   127	#
   128	# Catalog provider -> credential display text `_parse_opencode_auth` yields (map 1 of 2,
   129	# 012 AC-02). Used ONLY at the credential-set membership check. For the two pre-existing
   130	# pairs this string happens to coincide with the CLI argument below; for opencode-zen/
   131	# opencode-go it does not ("opencode zen"/"opencode go", two-token display text, vs the
   132	# single-token CLI ids in _OPENCODE_CLI_IDS) — re-measured live against `opencode auth
   133	# list --pure` (docs/specs/012-discovered-inventory/spec.md AC-02).
   134	_OPENCODE_PROVIDER_KEYS = {provider: spec.opencode_auth_key for provider, spec in PROVIDERS.items()}
   135	# Catalog provider -> CLI argument for `opencode models <id>` AND the `<id>/<model>` line
   136	# prefix `_parse_opencode_models` strips (map 2 of 2, 012 AC-02). Independently
   137	# addressable from _OPENCODE_PROVIDER_KEYS above: using the CLI-id value at the
   138	# credential-membership check would search the two-token display-name set for a
   139	# single-token string and report the pair absent on every machine (the defect this
   140	# two-map split fixes; PI_MODEL_MAP is explicitly the wrong precedent here — it
   141	# translates a model name within one already-matched provider, a different axis).
   142	_OPENCODE_CLI_IDS = {provider: spec.opencode_cli_id for provider, spec in PROVIDERS.items()}
   143	# ADR-0034 (019 PKG-1) re-measurement, live, 2026-08-10 (opencode 1.18.14): `opencode
   144	# auth list --pure` shows 4 real credentials -- `opencode-go`, `openai`, `github-copilot`,
   145	# `opencode` (auth.json keys). `github-copilot` is authenticated but `opencode models
   146	# github-copilot --pure` (even with `--refresh`) answers `Error: Provider not found:
   147	# github-copilot` -- no CLI id verifies it, so NO pair is added for it here (M-1, fail-
   148	# closed: never hardcode an unverified CLI id, never derive one by a display-name-to-
   149	# CLI-id heuristic like "replace the space with a hyphen" -- that would have produced
   150	# the WRONG id for `opencode-go` itself, "opencode go" -> "opencode-go" only works by
   151	# coincidence for that one pair and fails silently for anything else). The opencode
   152	# `openai` provider (OAuth ChatGPT) is NOT a new provider either -- it is the display/CLI
   153	# name this table ALREADY maps to `openai-codex` (M-2); no duplicate catalog provider is
   154	# added for it. Detection is structural, not a separate code path: `github-copilot`
   155	# never appears in `_PAIR_COMMANDS`, so `_probe_pairs` never even queries it -- the rest
   156	# of the inventory is never at risk of an unverified id leaking in. `--route-doctor`
   157	# (PKG-2, ADR-0035) is the surface that will report "detected, unlistable" to a human;
   158	# nothing here needs to change for that report to be accurate.
   159	# The audited pair table is the single source of runtime/provider compatibility:
   160	# a pair absent here can never authenticate, list models, or appear in identities.
   161	# 012 AC-01: two OpenCode-lane pairs added, `opencode-zen`/`opencode-go` — real,
   162	# currently-authenticated OpenCode providers that were simply outside this table
   163	# (see docs/specs/012-discovered-inventory/spec.md AC-01). No other runtime gains a
   164	# pair; codex/claude-code/pi have no client for either and none is implied here.
   165	# Repair F-05 (012 repair): the opencode-lane argv is DERIVED from _OPENCODE_CLI_IDS
   166	# above, never a second hardcoded copy of the CLI id — a future change to one map with
   167	# the other left stale would otherwise silently leave a pair absent, undetected by any
   168	# test or the live gate (both only ever exercise whichever id `_PAIR_COMMANDS` itself
   169	# carries).
   170	_PAIR_COMMANDS = {
   171	    ("codex", "openai-codex"): (("codex", "login", "status"),),
   172	    ("claude-code", "anthropic"): (("claude", "auth", "status", "--json"),),
   173	    **{("opencode", provider): (("opencode", "auth", "list", "--pure"), ("opencode", "models", cli_id, "--pure"))
   174	       for provider, cli_id in _OPENCODE_CLI_IDS.items()},
   175	    ("pi", "openai-codex"): (pi_pinned_argv("--list-models"),),
   176	    ("pi", "anthropic"): (pi_pinned_argv("--list-models"),),
   177	}
   178	# 012 AC-08: billing-model distinction, curated per provider — NEVER a routes.v1.toml row
   179	# field (the row schema is closed, required_keys/optional_keys below; an extra key raises
   180	# CATALOG_INVALID for every provider, existing two included). OpenCode Go is a monthly
   181	# subscription; OpenCode Zen is metered/API-key (user clarification, logged at
   182	# ai/state/decisions-log.jsonl, slug opencode-zen-go-billing-model-distinto-no-mismo-pool).
   183	# ADR-0035 (019 PKG-2): completed to cover every DISCOVERABLE_PROVIDERS member --
   184	# `openai-codex`/`anthropic` are the curated subscription lanes (Codex CLI login, Claude
   185	# Code OAuth), `opencode-go` stays subscription, `opencode-zen` stays the sole metered
   186	# entry. This is the day `billing_rank` (below) starts reading it -- the previous "no
   187	# weighting/selection logic reads this map yet" note is retired.
   188	# ADR-0042 (022 PKG-1): derives from `PROVIDERS` now, same values as the pre-ADR-0042 literal.
   189	PROVIDER_BILLING_KIND = {provider: spec.billing_kind for provider, spec in PROVIDERS.items()}
   190	# ADR-0035 AC-12: the FREE-model convention is the `-free` suffix -- the exact same
   191	# pattern `inference._FAST_HINTS` already treats as a signal, never a second, competing
   192	# definition of "free" invented here.
   193	_FREE_MODEL_SUFFIX = re.compile(r"-free(\b|$)")
   194	
   195	
   196	def billing_rank(provider: str, model: str) -> int:
   197	    """ADR-0035 (AC-12): pure order-only signal, `0` (cheap: subscription or a free-suffixed
   198	    model) or `1` (metered, or a provider this map does not even know -- fail-closed toward
   199	    the expensive rank, never toward the cheap one for an unrecognized provider). Consulted
   200	    ONLY by `service.route`'s sort key, strictly between `TIER_ORDER` and `_bias_rank`
   201	    (ADR-0035 d.3) -- it can never affect which candidates reach the sort, only the order
   202	    among candidates that already survived every hard exclusion."""
   203	    if PROVIDER_BILLING_KIND.get(provider) == "subscription":
   204	        return 0
   205	    if isinstance(model, str) and _FREE_MODEL_SUFFIX.search(model):
   206	        return 0
   207	    return 1
   208	_ANSI = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")
   209	
   210	
   211	def allowed_probe(runtime: str, provider: str) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...] | None:
   212	    """The closed, exact argv list for a pair; None for any unaudited pair."""
   213	    return _PAIR_COMMANDS.get((runtime, provider))
   214	
   215	
   216	# ADR-0042 (022 PKG-1/PKG-2): provider -> `[catalog]` TOML key, derived from `PROVIDERS`
   217	# (same values as the pre-ADR-0042 inline literal `_configured_models` used to carry). Site
   218	# (2) of the five-site lockstep `resolve_ceiling` documents below -- the other four sites
   219	# (the `[catalog]` table itself, `build_snapshot`'s own comprehension, and the two
   220	# `models_config.py` sites) are a DIFFERENT, wider concern (012 AC-04: whether a TOML key
   221	# exists and round-trips through `load_config`/`emit` at all) and are out of this package's
   222	# scope -- only this key map derives from the single provider registry.
   223	_CATALOG_KEYS = {provider: spec.catalog_key for provider, spec in PROVIDERS.items()}
   224	
   225	
   226	def resolve_ceiling(config: dict, provider: str) -> tuple[str, set[str] | None]:
   227	    """ADR-0042 (022 PKG-2): the tri-state ceiling every one of this file's three
   228	    consumers (`_probe_pairs`, `_read_probe_cache`, `build_snapshot`) reads through now,
   229	    replacing the two-state `_configured_models` this function inlined and removed. That
   230	    function only ever returned a bare `set()`, which collapsed two genuinely different
   231	    TOML shapes into the identical value: "no `[catalog]` key at all for this provider"
   232	    and "curated as an explicit empty allowlist". The FIRST must mean "no ceiling, the
   233	    live probe result is the routable set"; the SECOND must mean "never route this
   234	    provider, no matter what the probe shows" -- conflating them is exactly why
   235	    `_probe_pairs`'s `if not allowed: continue` skipped a provider ENTIRELY the moment its
   236	    `[catalog]` key was absent, which in turn forced editing `models.toml` to add any new
   237	    provider (the defect this whole package exists to remove).
   238	
   239	    Three states, a pure reflection of the literal TOML shape under `[catalog].<key>`:
   240	      - key absent from `[catalog]` entirely (or `provider` has no dedicated key at all,
   241	        e.g. a future `PROVIDERS` entry added without one) => `("auto", None)` -- no
   242	        ceiling; whatever the live probe reports for this provider IS the routable set,
   243	        unfiltered by any curated allowlist.
   244	      - key present as `[]` => `("veto", set())` -- curated, explicit "never route this
   245	        provider", regardless of what the live probe would otherwise show.
   246	      - key present as a non-empty list => `("curated", set-of-ids)` -- the audited
   247	        ceiling every model must be a member of (today's only behavior, unchanged).
   248	
   249	    Same five-site lockstep `_configured_models` used to document, site (2) of 5: (1)
   250	    `models.toml`'s `[catalog]` table itself; (2) this key map (`_CATALOG_KEYS` above); (3)
   251	    `build_snapshot`'s own `configured_models` comprehension (below); (4)
   252	    `models_config.load_config`'s optional-key validation loop for the same two TOML keys
   253	    (accepts `[]` now, AC-04); (5) `models_config.emit`'s preservation loop for the same
   254	    two keys (round-trips absence AND `[]`, never inventing either).
   255	
   256	    Per ADR-0042 (022 PKG-2, decision recorded by Federico 2026-08-12, spec AC-04): in
   257	    PRACTICE only `opencode-zen`/`opencode-go` ever reach the "auto"/"veto" branches --
   258	    `models_config.load_config` dies if `[catalog].claude`/`.codex` are absent or empty, so
   259	    `openai-codex`/`anthropic` are structurally guaranteed "curated" by the time any caller
   260	    here has a validated config. `resolve_ceiling` itself stays provider-agnostic and
   261	    enforces nothing about WHICH providers may reach which state -- that restriction lives
   262	    at the validation layer (`models_config.py`) on purpose, same split ADR-0042 already
   263	    documents for the rest of this registry (billing-kind logic never bleeding into
   264	    provider identity; here, "which providers get tri-state" never bleeding into "what a
   265	    given TOML shape means").
   266	    """
   267	    key = _CATALOG_KEYS.get(provider)
   268	    if key is None or key not in config.get("catalog", {}):
   269	        return ("auto", None)
   270	    models = config["catalog"][key]
   271	    if not isinstance(models, (list, tuple)):
   272	        return ("auto", None)  # defensive only -- load_config already dies on this shape
   273	    cleaned = {item for item in models if isinstance(item, str) and item}
   274	    if not cleaned:
   275	        return ("veto", set())
   276	    return ("curated", cleaned)
   277	
   278	
   279	def _clean_lines(text: str) -> list[str]:
   280	    return [line.strip() for line in _ANSI.sub("", text).splitlines() if line.strip()]
   281	
   282	
   283	def _parse_codex_login(stdout: str, stderr: str = "") -> bool:
   284	    """`codex login status` prints a plain-text state line (observed on stderr), never JSON."""
   285	    lines = _clean_lines(stdout) or _clean_lines(stderr)
   286	    return bool(lines) and lines[0].startswith("Logged in")
   287	
   288	
   289	def _parse_claude_auth(stdout: str) -> bool:
   290	    """`claude auth status --json` is a JSON object; only loggedIn is read and the raw doc is discarded."""
   291	    doc = json.loads(stdout)
   292	    return isinstance(doc, dict) and doc.get("loggedIn") is True
   293	
   294	
   295	def _parse_opencode_auth(stdout: str) -> set[str]:
   296	    """`opencode auth list --pure` prints decorated credential rows, one provider name per
   297	    bullet. ADR-0034 (AC-07): only `●`/`*` mark a REAL, usable credential; `○` is opencode's
   298	    own "pending/incomplete" marker (e.g. an invitation not yet accepted, a session that
   299	    needs re-auth) and must never count as authenticated -- treating it as live would widen
   300	    the audited set past what is actually usable, the opposite of fail-closed. Live-measured
   301	    2026-08-10 (opencode 1.18.14): every row on this machine is `●`, so this fix has no
   302	    observable effect on today's real inventory -- it closes the defect regardless."""
   303	    providers = set()
   304	    for line in _clean_lines(stdout):
   305	        if line.startswith("Error"):
   306	            raise RoutingError("PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED")
   307	        if line.startswith(("●", "*")):
   308	            body = line.lstrip("●* ").strip()
   309	            if body:
   310	                # The trailing word is the credential method (api/oauth); the rest is the provider name.
   311	                name = body.rsplit(None, 1)[0] if len(body.split()) > 1 else body
   312	                providers.add(name.strip().lower())
   313	    return providers
   314	
   315	
   316	def _parse_opencode_models(stdout: str, provider_key: str) -> set[str]:
   317	    """`opencode models <provider> --pure` prints `<provider>/<model>` lines; errors exit 0, so text is authoritative."""
   318	    models = set()
   319	    for line in _clean_lines(stdout):
   320	        if line.startswith("Error"):
   321	            raise RoutingError("PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED")
   322	        prefix = provider_key + "/"
   323	        if not line.startswith(prefix):
   324	            raise RoutingError("PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED")
   325	        model = line[len(prefix):].strip()
   326	        if model:
   327	            models.add(model)
   328	    return models
   329	
   330	
   331	def _parse_pi_models(stdout: str, provider: str) -> set[str]:
   332	    """`pi --list-models` prints a `provider  model  ...` column table (header row first,
   333	    columns whitespace-separated); only rows whose first column matches `provider` are
   334	    read. openai-codex ids are catalog-IDENTITY (returned verbatim, spike-verified);
   335	    anthropic raw ids are translated through PI_MODEL_MAP so callers intersect against the
   336	    SAME short-name vocabulary every other pair uses — a raw Pi id never leaks through
   337	    untranslated. An unrecognized header shape fails closed (PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED),
   338	    same discipline as the opencode parsers above."""
   339	    lines = _clean_lines(stdout)
   340	    if not lines or lines[0].split()[:2] != ["provider", "model"]:
   341	        raise RoutingError("PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED")
   342	    raw = {parts[1] for line in lines[1:] if len(parts := line.split()) >= 2 and parts[0] == provider}
   343	    if provider == "openai-codex":
   344	        return raw
   345	    return {short for short, canonical in PI_MODEL_MAP.get(provider, {}).items() if canonical in raw}
   346	
   347	
   348	def pi_auth_provider_keys() -> frozenset[str]:
   349	    """Read-only key-SET of `~/.pi/agent/auth.json` (spike Q2): provider NAMES only, never
   350	    token values, never logged. Any surprise (missing file, symlink, foreign shape, bad
   351	    JSON) is an empty set, fail-closed — this never raises and the caller never sees a
   352	    credential value, only whether a provider name is present as a key."""
   353	    path = Path.home() / ".pi/agent/auth.json"
   354	    try:
   355	        st = path.lstat()
   356	        if _stat.S_ISLNK(st.st_mode) or not _stat.S_ISREG(st.st_mode):
   357	            return frozenset()
   358	        doc = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   359	    except (OSError, ValueError):
   360	        return frozenset()
   361	    if not isinstance(doc, dict):
   362	        return frozenset()
   363	    return frozenset(key for key in doc if isinstance(key, str))
   364	
   365	
   366	def _binary_signature(name: str) -> str:
   367	    """ADR-0034 (AC-08), generalized by ADR-0043 (022 PKG-3, AC-07): path + mtime of
   368	    `name`'s resolved binary on PATH right now -- swapping the binary (upgrade,
   369	    reinstall, a different shim) invalidates every existing cache entry without a
   370	    manual version bump. Originally scoped to `opencode` alone; AC-07 extends the same
   371	    argument to `codex` and `claude` too -- any of the three CLI binaries can change how
   372	    it reads/writes its OWN credential store on a swap, so each binary's signature is
   373	    folded into `_cache_key` alongside its runtime's credential-file signature (below).
   374	    No subprocess here, just a PATH lookup and a stat -- safe to call from a pure
   375	    hashing function. Unresolvable is a fixed per-name sentinel that simply never
   376	    equals a real signature, so the cache is skipped (fail-closed), never fabricated as
   377	    a match."""
   378	    path = shutil.which(name)
   379	    if not path:
   380	        return f"{name}:absent"
   381	    try:
   382	        mtime_ns = os.stat(path).st_mtime_ns
   383	    except OSError:
   384	        return f"{name}:absent"
   385	    return f"{name}:{path}:{mtime_ns}"
   386	
   387	
   388	# ------------------------------------------------------------------ ADR-0043 (022 PKG-3)
   389	# Per-runtime credential signatures, AC-07: closes "de-auth invisible for up to
   390	# PROBE_CACHE_TTL (300s) in 3 of 4 runtimes" -- `_cache_key` used to fold in ONLY
   391	# opencode's live subprocess-based auth signature (`_live_opencode_auth_signature`
   392	# above). Every signature below is `stat`/local-read only -- ZERO new subprocesses
   393	# (the one subprocess this whole cache-key computation ever pays stays
   394	# `_live_opencode_auth_signature`, called by the CALLER and passed in, never invoked
   395	# from here -- see `test_adr0043_ac07_cache_key_never_spawns_a_subprocess_for_the_new_
   396	# signatures` in tests/test_routing.py and evidence/P3-implementer.md's proof). Same
   397	# security discipline as `pi_auth_provider_keys` throughout (AC-09): `lstat` (never a
   398	# symlink), a regular file, this uid, ONLY named fields ever read -- never a whole-file
   399	# hash or mtime, because `~/.claude/.credentials.json` also holds an unrelated
   400	# `mcpOAuth` block (today a Vercel token, live-measured) that rotates on ITS OWN MCP
   401	# refresh cycle with zero relation to the Claude Code provider credential; hashing the
   402	# file or its mtime would rotate this signature on every MCP token refresh, defeating
   403	# the cache for a reason that has nothing to do with what this signature exists to
   404	# track. Every value is hashed before being folded into `_cache_key` -- never logged,
   405	# never placed in an envelope, never a raw credential value.
   406	
   407	
   408	def _read_credential_json(path: Path) -> dict | None:
   409	    """Shared fail-closed read for a local per-runtime credential file: `lstat` (never
   410	    a symlink), a regular file, owned by this uid, valid JSON, a dict -- ANY surprise is
   411	    `None`, never raised, same discipline as `pi_auth_provider_keys` above. The caller
   412	    reads only specific NAMED fields from the result; this function itself never
   413	    inspects field values, so widening what a signature reads is always a one-line,
   414	    auditable change at the call site, never here."""
   415	    try:
   416	        st = path.lstat()
   417	        if _stat.S_ISLNK(st.st_mode) or not _stat.S_ISREG(st.st_mode) or st.st_uid != os.getuid():
   418	            return None
   419	        doc = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   420	    except (OSError, ValueError):
   421	        return None
   422	    return doc if isinstance(doc, dict) else None
   423	
   424	
   425	def _codex_auth_signature() -> str:
   426	    """AC-07/AC-09: codex's per-runtime signature -- `~/.codex/auth.json`, ONLY
   427	    `tokens.account_id` (real account identity; codex is the one runtime of the three
   428	    whose credential file carries one, ADR-0043) and whether `OPENAI_API_KEY` is
   429	    present (a boolean, never the key's own value). NEVER `tokens.access_token`/
   430	    `tokens.refresh_token`/`tokens.id_token`/`last_refresh` -- token material and its
   431	    timestamp, which rotate on every refresh (AC-08 property 1: a signature built from
   432	    a rotating field would miss the cache on every routine OAuth renewal). File absent,
   433	    foreign-owned, a symlink, or unreadable -- fail-closed to the empty string, which
   434	    never equals a real signature, so the cache is simply skipped (a fresh probe runs),
   435	    never fabricated as a match (AC-08 property 2: this is what makes a real logout/
   436	    delete visible immediately)."""
   437	    doc = _read_credential_json(Path.home() / ".codex/auth.json")
   438	    if doc is None:
   439	        return ""
   440	    tokens = doc.get("tokens")
   441	    account_id = tokens.get("account_id") if isinstance(tokens, dict) else None
   442	    account_id = account_id if isinstance(account_id, str) else ""
   443	    has_api_key = isinstance(doc.get("OPENAI_API_KEY"), str)
   444	    material = f"account_id={account_id}|api_key_present={has_api_key}"
   445	    return hashlib.sha256(material.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
   446	
   447	
   448	def _claude_code_auth_signature() -> str:
   449	    """AC-07/AC-09: claude-code's per-runtime signature -- `~/.claude/.credentials.json`,
   450	    ONLY the three `claudeAiOauth` fields measured NON-rotating across a refresh
   451	    (`scopes`, `subscriptionType`, `rateLimitTier` -- attributes of the OAuth grant/plan
   452	    itself, not of the token instance; evidence/P3-implementer.md). NEVER
   453	    `accessToken`/`refreshToken`/`expiresAt`/`refreshTokenExpiresAt` (token material,
   454	    AC-09: "hashear el refreshToken no se hace"), and NEVER the file's OTHER top-level
   455	    key `mcpOAuth` (the trap this module's own section docstring names).
   456	    ADR-0043's declared limit: `claudeAiOauth` carries NO account-identity field (unlike
   457	    codex's `tokens.account_id`) -- this signature therefore detects PRESENCE/ABSENCE
   458	    (logout, deletion, file absent) but never a same-plan account swap without an
   459	    intervening logout. Closing that gap would mean hashing `refreshToken`, which this
   460	    package explicitly does not do."""
   461	    doc = _read_credential_json(Path.home() / ".claude/.credentials.json")
   462	    if doc is None:
   463	        return ""
   464	    oauth = doc.get("claudeAiOauth")
   465	    if not isinstance(oauth, dict):
   466	        return ""
   467	    parts = [str(oauth.get(field)) for field in ("scopes", "subscriptionType", "rateLimitTier")]
   468	    return hashlib.sha256("|".join(parts).encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
   469	
   470	
   471	def _pi_auth_signature() -> str:
   472	    """AC-07: pi's per-runtime signature folds `pi_auth_provider_keys()` (already
   473	    stat/local-read only, zero subprocess, spike Q2 discipline -- untouched by this
   474	    package) with `PI_PINNED_VERSION` (the EXACT pi release the probe invokes, above) --
   475	    a version bump is itself credential-relevant: a different pinned pi release could
   476	    read/write a differently-shaped auth.json. Provider NAMES only (never a token
   477	    value), hashed before folding into `_cache_key`."""
   478	    names = ",".join(sorted(pi_auth_provider_keys()))
   479	    return hashlib.sha256(f"{names}|{PI_PINNED_VERSION}".encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
   480	
   481	
   482	def _live_opencode_auth_signature(timeout: float) -> str:
   483	    """ADR-0034 (AC-08): a cheap, ALWAYS-fresh (never itself cached) read of `opencode
   484	    auth list --pure` -- credential NAMES only, never a model listing, never persisted
   485	    to disk on its own -- folded into the cache key so the authenticated-provider set is
   486	    genuinely re-checked on every composition that consults the cache, instead of trusted
   487	    from whatever it was when the cache was last written. Any surprise (missing binary,
   488	    timeout, non-zero exit, parse failure) is the empty signature, fail-closed: an
   489	    unreadable auth state simply differs from a real one, so the cache is missed and a
   490	    full fresh probe runs -- it can never fabricate a match."""
   491	    probe_env = dict(os.environ, CI="1", NO_COLOR="1", TERM="dumb")
   492	    try:
   493	        completed = subprocess.run(("opencode", "auth", "list", "--pure"), stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,
   494	                                   stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True,
   495	                                   timeout=timeout, check=False, env=probe_env)
   496	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   497	        return ""
   498	    if completed.returncode != 0:
   499	        return ""
   500	    try:
   501	        return ",".join(sorted(_parse_opencode_auth(completed.stdout)))
   502	    except RoutingError:
   503	        return ""
   504	
   505	
   506	def _cache_key(config: dict, auth_signature: str = "") -> str:
   507	    """ADR-0006 base key (uid + canonical catalog/routing config), the cache schema
   508	    version, always; extended by ADR-0034 AC-08 with the resolved `opencode` binary's
   509	    path+mtime, and by ADR-0043 (022 PKG-3, AC-07) with EVERY runtime's own
   510	    stat/local-read-only credential signature (`_codex_auth_signature`,
   511	    `_claude_code_auth_signature`, `_pi_auth_signature`) plus the `codex`/`claude`
   512	    binary signatures (`_binary_signature`, generalized from the opencode-only one) --
   513	    none of these six calls does any I/O beyond a stat/local read, so this function
   514	    stays pure/no-subprocess for every existing direct caller (proven by
   515	    `test_adr0043_ac07_cache_key_never_spawns_a_subprocess_for_the_new_signatures`,
   516	    tests/test_routing.py). `auth_signature` -- the caller-supplied, freshly-read,
   517	    normalized opencode-authenticated-provider set, the ONE genuinely subprocess-backed
   518	    signal in this whole key -- is folded in only when the caller actually has one.
   519	    `probe_inventory` is the only production caller that supplies a non-empty
   520	    `auth_signature` (see `_live_opencode_auth_signature` above); every pre-existing
   521	    direct caller (including tests) keeps calling this with the same two-argument shape
   522	    it always had and is unaffected. The ENTIRE key changes when ANY one runtime's
   523	    credential state changes -- the probe-cache document covers every runtime's pairs
   524	    at once, so a credential change in one runtime correctly forces a fresh probe of
   525	    ALL pairs on the next decision, never a partial/stale mix."""
   526	    canon = json.dumps({"catalog": config.get("catalog", {}), "routing": config.get("routing", {})},
   527	                       sort_keys=True, default=str)
   528	    parts = "\n".join((str(os.getuid()), canon, str(_CACHE_SCHEMA_VERSION),
   529	                       _binary_signature("opencode"), _binary_signature("codex"), _binary_signature("claude"),
   530	                       _codex_auth_signature(), _claude_code_auth_signature(), _pi_auth_signature(),
   531	                       auth_signature))
   532	    return hashlib.sha256(parts.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
   533	
   534	
   535	def _validate_cache_dir(root: Path) -> bool:
   536	    """Same private-directory discipline as the store: no symlink, this uid's 0700 dir.
   537	
   538	    Never creates or chmods anything; an unvalidated root just means the cache
   539	    is skipped (read AND write) and every pair is probed fresh (SEC-A03/F06).
   540	    """
   541	    try:
   542	        st = root.lstat()
   543	    except OSError:
   544	        return False
   545	    return (_stat.S_ISDIR(st.st_mode) and not _stat.S_ISLNK(st.st_mode)
   546	            and st.st_uid == os.getuid() and _stat.S_IMODE(st.st_mode) == 0o700)
   547	
   548	
   549	def _read_probe_cache(cache_root, key: str, now: float, config: dict):
   550	    """Valid cache or None; anything unexpected is ignored fail-closed (fresh probes run).
   551	
   552	    Only PAIRS PRESENT are ever positive (F06: negatives are never persisted,
   553	    so a transient failure costs one retry, never the full TTL). Every entry
   554	    is re-checked against the live `resolve_ceiling` state on read (F09), so a
   555	    stale-but-key-matching cache can never widen the audited set. ADR-0042 (022 PKG-2):
   556	    the three tri-states matter here as much as at `_probe_pairs` -- a "veto" provider
   557	    is dropped outright (never served from cache, however it got written); a "curated"
   558	    provider is re-intersected exactly like before; an "auto" provider has NO ceiling to
   559	    intersect against, so the cached models are kept as-is. Naively re-intersecting
   560	    against `set()` in "auto" mode (the pre-tri-state behavior applied blindly) would
   561	    leave every auto-mode pair's cached entry EMPTY on every read, defeating the cache
   562	    entirely -- the caller would re-probe on every single decision (up to
   563	    `PI_PROBE_MIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` for pi). See `test_read_probe_cache_auto_mode_is_not_
   564	    always_empty` in tests/test_routing.py, which exists specifically to catch that
   565	    regression.
   566	    """
   567	    root = Path(cache_root)
   568	    if not _validate_cache_dir(root):
   569	        return None
   570	    try:
   571	        path = root / "probe-cache.json"
   572	        st = path.lstat()
   573	        if _stat.S_ISLNK(st.st_mode) or not _stat.S_ISREG(st.st_mode) or st.st_uid != os.getuid() or _stat.S_IMODE(st.st_mode) != 0o600:
   574	            return None
   575	        doc = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   576	        if (not isinstance(doc, dict) or doc.get("key") != key
   577	                or not isinstance(doc.get("at"), (int, float)) or isinstance(doc.get("at"), bool)
   578	                or doc["at"] > now or now - doc["at"] > PROBE_CACHE_TTL
   579	                or not isinstance(doc.get("pairs"), dict)):
   580	            return None
   581	        out: dict[tuple[str, str], set[str]] = {}
   582	        for name, models in doc["pairs"].items():
   583	            runtime, _, provider = str(name).partition("|")
   584	            if (runtime, provider) not in _PAIR_COMMANDS or not isinstance(models, list) or not all(isinstance(m, str) for m in models):
   585	                return None
   586	            state, ceiling = resolve_ceiling(config, provider)
   587	            if state == "veto":
   588	                continue  # never served from cache, no matter what is on disk (AC-05 layer 2)
   589	            # "auto": no ceiling exists to re-intersect against -- keeping `set()` here
   590	            # (the naive port of the pre-tri-state one-liner) would empty every auto-mode
   591	            # entry on every single read, defeating the cache (see docstring above).
   592	            kept = set(models) if state == "auto" else (set(models) & ceiling)
   593	            if kept:  # empty after filtering ⇒ treated as absent, retried like any negative
   594	                out[(runtime, provider)] = kept
   595	        return out
   596	    except (OSError, ValueError):
   597	        return None
   598	
   599	
   600	def _write_probe_cache(cache_root, key: str, result: dict, now: float) -> None:
   601	    """Atomic 0600 tmp+rename under the managed root; content is redacted pair->models only."""
   602	    root = Path(cache_root)
   603	    if not _validate_cache_dir(root):
   604	        return  # the store owns root creation; never create or chmod it from here
   605	    tmp = root / f".probe-cache.{os.getpid()}.tmp"
   606	    doc = {"key": key, "at": now,
   607	           "pairs": {f"{runtime}|{provider}": sorted(models) for (runtime, provider), models in result.items()}}
   608	    old = os.umask(0o077)
   609	    try:
   610	        tmp.write_text(json.dumps(doc, sort_keys=True), encoding="utf-8")
   611	        os.chmod(tmp, 0o600)
   612	        os.replace(tmp, root / "probe-cache.json")
   613	    except OSError:
   614	        try: tmp.unlink()
   615	        except OSError: pass
   616	    finally:
   617	        os.umask(old)
   618	
   619	
   620	def prune_legacy_probe_cache(legacy_root) -> bool:
   621	    """AC-10 (022 PKG-3): a single probe cache lives at the store's cache root now
   622	    (`RoutingStore.ensure_cache_root()`, `~/.local/state/set-agentes/routing-v2`) --
   623	    this removes the STALE SIBLING file at the old, divergent location
   624	    (`STATE_DIR/probe-cache.json`, what `--route-doctor`/`--doctor-all`/the 'Estado
   625	    general' panel used to read while the decision path already read/wrote the
   626	    routing-v2 one -- two caches confirmed diverging live, ADR-0043).
   627	
   628	    Same security discipline as `_write_probe_cache`, mirrored exactly: the directory
   629	    must pass `_validate_cache_dir` (0700, this uid, no symlink) and the file itself
   630	    must independently be a regular, this-uid file (never following a symlink) before
   631	    it is ever unlinked -- any surprise leaves it untouched (fail-closed: an
   632	    unrecognized foreign object is never deleted). `STATE_DIR` also holds
   633	    `config.toml`/`model-preference.toml` and other real state -- this function NEVER
   634	    touches the directory itself or any other file in it, only this one legacy
   635	    `probe-cache.json`. Returns True only when a file was actually removed (for the
   636	    caller's own best-effort bookkeeping); False for "nothing to prune" and every
   637	    fail-closed branch alike -- the caller never needs to distinguish them, pruning is
   638	    always best-effort."""
   639	    root = Path(legacy_root)
   640	    if not _validate_cache_dir(root):
   641	        return False
   642	    path = root / "probe-cache.json"
   643	    try:
   644	        st = path.lstat()
   645	    except OSError:
   646	        return False
   647	    if _stat.S_ISLNK(st.st_mode) or not _stat.S_ISREG(st.st_mode) or st.st_uid != os.getuid():
   648	        return False
   649	    try:
   650	        path.unlink()
   651	    except OSError:
   652	        return False
   653	    return True
   654	
   655	
   656	def _run_cached(ran, argv, timeout, env):
   657	    """Subprocess run for `argv`, memoized in the caller's `ran` dict (shared across pairs
   658	    in one probe call, e.g. the single `opencode auth list --pure` all opencode pairs
   659	    reuse). Returns the CompletedProcess, or None on any OSError/timeout — never raises."""
   660	    if argv not in ran:
   661	        try:
   662	            ran[argv] = subprocess.run(argv, stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
   663	                                       text=True, timeout=timeout, check=False, env=env)
   664	        except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   665	            ran[argv] = None
   666	    return ran[argv]
   667	
   668	
   669	def _probe_pairs(config: dict, pairs, timeout: float, ran=None) -> dict[tuple[str, str], set[str]]:
   670	    """Fresh, uncached, pair-scoped subprocess observation for exactly `pairs`.
   671	
   672	    Any surprise — missing binary, timeout, nonzero exit, unexpected shape,
   673	    error text — makes only that pair unavailable (never raises). A pair
   674	    without any resulting model is simply absent from the returned dict:
   675	    negative results are never a first-class cached value (F06).
   676	    """
   677	    result: dict[tuple[str, str], set[str]] = {}
   678	    # Identical argvs across pairs (e.g. `opencode auth list` for both opencode pairs) run once.
   679	    ran = {} if ran is None else ran
   680	    # Non-interactive probe environment: opencode's TUI writer blocks forever on a
   681	    # non-TTY stdout unless CI/TERM signal a dumb terminal (observed 2026-07-26).
   682	    probe_env = dict(os.environ, CI="1", NO_COLOR="1", TERM="dumb")
   683	    for pair in pairs:
   684	        runtime, provider = pair
   685	        commands = _PAIR_COMMANDS[pair]
   686	        # ADR-0042 (022 PKG-2, AC-04): "veto" is dropped before any subprocess runs at
   687	        # all -- same early-exit position the pre-tri-state `if not allowed: continue`
   688	        # had, so a curated-empty provider still costs nothing to probe. "auto" (no
   689	        # `[catalog]` key for this provider) used to fall into that SAME branch (an empty
   690	        # `set()` is falsy too) and get skipped just the same -- exactly the defect this
   691	        # package removes: `allowed` being `None` below is deliberately NOT treated as
   692	        # falsy-skip; only genuine credential/parse failure further down drops the pair.
   693	        state, allowed = resolve_ceiling(config, provider)
   694	        if state == "veto":
   695	            continue
   696	        # PKG-N02: only the pi pairs get the cold-pnpm floor; every other pair keeps the
   697	        # caller's own timeout unchanged.
   698	        pair_timeout = max(timeout, PI_PROBE_MIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS) if runtime == "pi" else timeout
   699	        if runtime == "opencode":
   700	            # 012 AC-02: the credential-set check (map 1) and the models-listing CLI
   701	            # id/prefix (map 2) are two independently addressable lookups, never one.
   702	            # Repair F-06 (012 repair, medium, +69%/+10.5s measured without this fix):
   703	            # map 1's membership check runs BEFORE the second, more expensive `opencode
   704	            # models <id>` call, not after both commands already ran — a machine missing
   705	            # a given OpenCode-lane subscription now never pays that pair's models-call
   706	            # latency at all, instead of always paying it only to discard the result.
   707	            auth_result = _run_cached(ran, commands[0], pair_timeout, probe_env)
   708	            if auth_result is None or auth_result.returncode != 0:
   709	                continue
   710	            try:
   711	                provider_key = _OPENCODE_PROVIDER_KEYS[provider]
   712	                credentials = _parse_opencode_auth(auth_result.stdout)
   713	            except (RoutingError, ValueError, KeyError, IndexError, TypeError):
   714	                continue
   715	            if provider_key not in credentials:
   716	                continue
   717	            models_result = _run_cached(ran, commands[1], pair_timeout, probe_env)
   718	            if models_result is None or models_result.returncode != 0:
   719	                continue
   720	            try:
   721	                cli_id = _OPENCODE_CLI_IDS[provider]
   722	                parsed = _parse_opencode_models(models_result.stdout, cli_id)
   723	            except (RoutingError, ValueError, KeyError, IndexError, TypeError):
   724	                continue
   725	            # AC-05 layer 3 is unaffected by "auto": billing_rank's fail-closed-to-
   726	            # expensive default already applies to any provider PROVIDER_BILLING_KIND
   727	            # does not classify as "subscription" -- ceiling state never enters that map.
   728	            models = parsed if state == "auto" else (allowed & parsed)
   729	        else:
   730	            completed, failed = [], False
   731	            for argv in commands:
   732	                completed_item = _run_cached(ran, argv, pair_timeout, probe_env)
   733	                if completed_item is None:
   734	                    failed = True; break
   735	                completed.append(completed_item)
   736	            if failed or any(item.returncode != 0 for item in completed):
   737	                continue
   738	            try:
   739	                if runtime == "codex":
   740	                    # ADR-0042 AC-04: codex/claude-code never see state == "auto" here --
   741	                    # `models_config.load_config` dies if [catalog].codex/.claude are
   742	                    # absent or empty, so `allowed` is always the curated set by the time
   743	                    # a validated config reaches this function (see resolve_ceiling's
   744	                    # own docstring). Neither runtime has a models-LISTING command to
   745	                    # fall back on if it ever were "auto" (only a login-status boolean).
   746	                    models = allowed if _parse_codex_login(completed[0].stdout, completed[0].stderr) else set()
   747	                elif runtime == "claude-code":
   748	                    models = allowed if _parse_claude_auth(completed[0].stdout) else set()
   749	                else:  # pi
   750	                    # Belt-and-suspenders (T-305, spike Q2): the auth.json key-set is a cheap,
   751	                    # non-subprocess signal alongside the naturally fail-closed column-parse
   752	                    # below (an unauthenticated provider simply has no rows in --list-models).
   753	                    if provider not in pi_auth_provider_keys():
   754	                        continue
   755	                    # Same guarantee as codex/claude-code above: pi's two pairs are both
   756	                    # openai-codex/anthropic, always curated by validation.
   757	                    models = allowed & _parse_pi_models(completed[0].stdout, provider)
   758	            except (RoutingError, ValueError, KeyError, IndexError, TypeError):
   759	                continue
   760	        if models:

-- subprocess references --

391:# opencode's live subprocess-based auth signature (`_live_opencode_auth_signature`

473:    stat/local-read only, zero subprocess, spike Q2 discipline -- untouched by this

494:                                   stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True,
496:    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):

 def pi_pinned_argv(*args: str) -> tuple[str, ...]:
     """Exact-version-pinned `pi` invocation shared by the probe (here) and set_agents_spawn.
 
     Deliberately NO `--` separator before `args`: live QA (2026-07-27) found `pnpm dlx
     --package <pkg> pi -- <args>` forwards that `--` VERBATIM into pi's own argument
     parser instead of stripping it — `pi --version`/`pi --list-models` tolerate the stray
     token (both short-circuit on the first recognized early-exit flag regardless of what
     else is on the line), which silently masked the bug for the probe/doctor calls, but a
     real spawn invocation (`--model ... --print ...`) fails hard with `Unknown option: --`.
     `pnpm dlx --package <pkg> <bin> <args...>` (no separator) is the correct form."""
     return ("pnpm", "dlx", "--package", f"{PI_PACKAGE}@{PI_PINNED_VERSION}", "pi", *args)

 # Repair SEC-001 (panel RP-01, security-auditor, critical, 012 repair): AC-07's
 # family-collision guard originally keyed `seen` on the RAW `model` string, so the SAME
 # underlying model curated under two DIFFERENT ids for two different providers (not the

 # from this map, a model id is already its own canonical form — this never invents an
 # equivalence beyond what is explicitly curated here, same discipline as PROVIDER_BILLING_KIND.

 # name-translation table (a different purpose and lifecycle from a security guarantee —

 # Anthropic id models.toml's `[catalog].claude` allowlist curates: `fable` has no Pi

 # has no reason to cover — never derived from PI_MODEL_MAP or any other table with a
 # different purpose. The coherence test (test_sec002_... in tests/test_routing.py)

 # id actually present in `[catalog].opencode_zen`, so a future fifth Anthropic id added

 _ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL_EXTRA = {"fable": "claude-fable-5"}
 _ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL: dict[str, str] = {**PI_MODEL_MAP["anthropic"], **_ANTHROPIC_CANONICAL_EXTRA}

                         for provider in ("opencode-zen", "opencode-go")

 def canonical_model(provider: str, model: str) -> str:
     """Curated cross-provider model-identity normalization (repair SEC-001). Resolves a
     (provider, model) pair to a canonical id ONLY where CANONICAL_MODEL explicitly
     curates the two as the same underlying model; every other pair maps to its own
     `model` string unchanged (identity), so this can never widen equivalence beyond what
     a human curated. Consumed by `_check_family_collisions` below (layer 1) and by
     `service.py`'s REVIEW_MODEL_CONFLICT hard exclusion (layer 2, defense in depth)."""
     return CANONICAL_MODEL.get((provider, model), model)

 # ADR-0035 AC-12: the FREE-model convention is the `-free` suffix -- the exact same
 # pattern `inference._FAST_HINTS` already treats as a signal, never a second, competing
 # definition of "free" invented here.
 _FREE_MODEL_SUFFIX = re.compile(r"-free(\b|$)")
 
 
 def billing_rank(provider: str, model: str) -> int:
     """ADR-0035 (AC-12): pure order-only signal, `0` (cheap: subscription or a free-suffixed
     model) or `1` (metered, or a provider this map does not even know -- fail-closed toward
     the expensive rank, never toward the cheap one for an unrecognized provider). Consulted
     ONLY by `service.route`'s sort key, strictly between `TIER_ORDER` and `_bias_rank`
     (ADR-0035 d.3) -- it can never affect which candidates reach the sort, only the order
     among candidates that already survived every hard exclusion."""
     if PROVIDER_BILLING_KIND.get(provider) == "subscription":
         return 0
     if isinstance(model, str) and _FREE_MODEL_SUFFIX.search(model):
         return 0
     return 1
 _ANSI = re.compile(r"\x1b\[[0-9;]*m")

+# ADR-0042 (022 PKG-1/PKG-2): provider -> `[catalog]` TOML key, derived from `PROVIDERS`
+# (same values as the pre-ADR-0042 inline literal `_configured_models` used to carry). Site
+# (2) of the five-site lockstep `resolve_ceiling` documents below -- the other four sites
+# (the `[catalog]` table itself, `build_snapshot`'s own comprehension, and the two
+# `models_config.py` sites) are a DIFFERENT, wider concern (012 AC-04: whether a TOML key
+# exists and round-trips through `load_config`/`emit` at all) and are out of this package's
+# scope -- only this key map derives from the single provider registry.
+_CATALOG_KEYS = {provider: spec.catalog_key for provider, spec in PROVIDERS.items()}

+def resolve_ceiling(config: dict, provider: str) -> tuple[str, set[str] | None]:
+    """ADR-0042 (022 PKG-2): the tri-state ceiling every one of this file's three
+    consumers (`_probe_pairs`, `_read_probe_cache`, `build_snapshot`) reads through now,
+    replacing the two-state `_configured_models` this function inlined and removed. That
+    function only ever returned a bare `set()`, which collapsed two genuinely different
+    TOML shapes into the identical value: "no `[catalog]` key at all for this provider"
+    and "curated as an explicit empty allowlist". The FIRST must mean "no ceiling, the
+    live probe result is the routable set"; the SECOND must mean "never route this
+    provider, no matter what the probe shows" -- conflating them is exactly why
+    `_probe_pairs`'s `if not allowed: continue` skipped a provider ENTIRELY the moment its
+    `[catalog]` key was absent, which in turn forced editing `models.toml` to add any new
+    provider (the defect this whole package exists to remove).
+
+    Three states, a pure reflection of the literal TOML shape under `[catalog].<key>`:
+      - key absent from `[catalog]` entirely (or `provider` has no dedicated key at all,
+        e.g. a future `PROVIDERS` entry added without one) => `("auto", None)` -- no
+        ceiling; whatever the live probe reports for this provider IS the routable set,
+        unfiltered by any curated allowlist.
+      - key present as `[]` => `("veto", set())` -- curated, explicit "never route this
+        provider", regardless of what the live probe would otherwise show.
+      - key present as a non-empty list => `("curated", set-of-ids)` -- the audited
+        ceiling every model must be a member of (today's only behavior, unchanged).
+
+    Same five-site lockstep `_configured_models` used to document, site (2) of 5: (1)
+    `models.toml`'s `[catalog]` table itself; (2) this key map (`_CATALOG_KEYS` above); (3)
+    `build_snapshot`'s own `configured_models` comprehension (below); (4)
+    `models_config.load_config`'s optional-key validation loop for the same two TOML keys
+    (accepts `[]` now, AC-04); (5) `models_config.emit`'s preservation loop for the same
+    two keys (round-trips absence AND `[]`, never inventing either).
+
+    Per ADR-0042 (022 PKG-2, decision recorded by Federico 2026-08-12, spec AC-04): in
+    PRACTICE only `opencode-zen`/`opencode-go` ever reach the "auto"/"veto" branches --
+    `models_config.load_config` dies if `[catalog].claude`/`.codex` are absent or empty, so
+    `openai-codex`/`anthropic` are structurally guaranteed "curated" by the time any caller
+    here has a validated config. `resolve_ceiling` itself stays provider-agnostic and
+    enforces nothing about WHICH providers may reach which state -- that restriction lives
+    at the validation layer (`models_config.py`) on purpose, same split ADR-0042 already
+    documents for the rest of this registry (billing-kind logic never bleeding into
+    provider identity; here, "which providers get tri-state" never bleeding into "what a
+    given TOML shape means").
+    """
+    key = _CATALOG_KEYS.get(provider)
+    if key is None or key not in config.get("catalog", {}):
+        return ("auto", None)
+    models = config["catalog"][key]
     if not isinstance(models, (list, tuple)):
-        return set()
-    return {item for item in models if isinstance(item, str) and item}
+        return ("auto", None)  # defensive only -- load_config already dies on this shape
+    cleaned = {item for item in models if isinstance(item, str) and item}
+    if not cleaned:
+        return ("veto", set())
+    return ("curated", cleaned)
 
 
 def _clean_lines(text: str) -> list[str]:
     return [line.strip() for line in _ANSI.sub("", text).splitlines() if line.strip()]
 
 
 def _parse_codex_login(stdout: str, stderr: str = "") -> bool:
     """`codex login status` prints a plain-text state line (observed on stderr), never JSON."""
     lines = _clean_lines(stdout) or _clean_lines(stderr)
     return bool(lines) and lines[0].startswith("Logged in")
 
 
 def _parse_claude_auth(stdout: str) -> bool:
     """`claude auth status --json` is a JSON object; only loggedIn is read and the raw doc is discarded."""
     doc = json.loads(stdout)
     return isinstance(doc, dict) and doc.get("loggedIn") is True
 
 
 def _parse_opencode_auth(stdout: str) -> set[str]:
     """`opencode auth list --pure` prints decorated credential rows, one provider name per
     bullet. ADR-0034 (AC-07): only `●`/`*` mark a REAL, usable credential; `○` is opencode's
     own "pending/incomplete" marker (e.g. an invitation not yet accepted, a session that
     needs re-auth) and must never count as authenticated -- treating it as live would widen
     the audited set past what is actually usable, the opposite of fail-closed. Live-measured
     2026-08-10 (opencode 1.18.14): every row on this machine is `●`, so this fix has no
     observable effect on today's real inventory -- it closes the defect regardless."""
     providers = set()
     for line in _clean_lines(stdout):
         if line.startswith("Error"):
             raise RoutingError("PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED")
         if line.startswith(("●", "*")):
             body = line.lstrip("●* ").strip()
             if body:
                 # The trailing word is the credential method (api/oauth); the rest is the provider name.
                 name = body.rsplit(None, 1)[0] if len(body.split()) > 1 else body
                 providers.add(name.strip().lower())
     return providers
 
 
 def _parse_opencode_models(stdout: str, provider_key: str) -> set[str]:
     """`opencode models <provider> --pure` prints `<provider>/<model>` lines; errors exit 0, so text is authoritative."""
     models = set()
     for line in _clean_lines(stdout):
         if line.startswith("Error"):
             raise RoutingError("PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED")
         prefix = provider_key + "/"
         if not line.startswith(prefix):
             raise RoutingError("PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED")
         model = line[len(prefix):].strip()
         if model:
             models.add(model)
     return models
 
 
 def _parse_pi_models(stdout: str, provider: str) -> set[str]:
     """`pi --list-models` prints a `provider  model  ...` column table (header row first,
     columns whitespace-separated); only rows whose first column matches `provider` are
     read. openai-codex ids are catalog-IDENTITY (returned verbatim, spike-verified);
     anthropic raw ids are translated through PI_MODEL_MAP so callers intersect against the
     SAME short-name vocabulary every other pair uses — a raw Pi id never leaks through
     untranslated. An unrecognized header shape fails closed (PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED),
     same discipline as the opencode parsers above."""
     lines = _clean_lines(stdout)
     if not lines or lines[0].split()[:2] != ["provider", "model"]:
         raise RoutingError("PROVIDER_UNAUTHENTICATED")
     raw = {parts[1] for line in lines[1:] if len(parts := line.split()) >= 2 and parts[0] == provider}
     if provider == "openai-codex":
         return raw
     return {short for short, canonical in PI_MODEL_MAP.get(provider, {}).items() if canonical in raw}
 
 
 def pi_auth_provider_keys() -> frozenset[str]:
     """Read-only key-SET of `~/.pi/agent/auth.json` (spike Q2): provider NAMES only, never
     token values, never logged. Any surprise (missing file, symlink, foreign shape, bad
     JSON) is an empty set, fail-closed — this never raises and the caller never sees a
     credential value, only whether a provider name is present as a key."""
     path = Path.home() / ".pi/agent/auth.json"
     try:
         st = path.lstat()
         if _stat.S_ISLNK(st.st_mode) or not _stat.S_ISREG(st.st_mode):
             return frozenset()
         doc = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
     except (OSError, ValueError):
         return frozenset()
     if not isinstance(doc, dict):
         return frozenset()
     return frozenset(key for key in doc if isinstance(key, str))
